pBh_I$elC4	Typecloze	Cloze Grammar::Japanese::N5::01 「です」と基本の助詞	私{{c1::は::particle: topic}}学生です。	I am a student.	Form	は marks the topic, what the sentence is about: 私は 'as for me'. Written with the hiragana は but read 'wa' when it's a particle, a spelling kept from older Japanese. です is the polite 'is / am / are'.	Particle は is read 'wa', not 'ha'.	Polite (です)		JP-N5-01-001	Japanese::N5::grammar::wa-desu	Japanese::N5::grammar::wa-desu

Japanese has no articles and no plural endings, so 大きい町 covers 'a big city' and 'big cities' alike.		Polite (です)		JP-N5-01-002	Japanese::N5::grammar::wa-desu	Japanese::N5::grammar::wa-desu

It never changes for person or number, so 私は…です, 彼は…です and 私たちは…です all use the same form. The plain equivalent is だ. Form	でした is the polite past of です: 休みでした 'was a day off'. です has only two tenses, past and non-past, so 'yesterday was' and 'it has been' both use でした. Form	か after a question word asks an open question: 何ですか 'what is it?'. 何 is read なん before です, の and counters, and なに elsewhere (何を).	何 is なん before です: なんですか. Form	も replaces は, が or を and means 'also, too': 姉も 'my sister too'. It is never added on top of them (×姉はも). 大学生 = university student.	も replaces は, が and を; it never follows them. Form	も links the second sentence to the first: Yamada is a teacher as well. The thing in common (先生です) is repeated, as Japanese has no 'too' at the end of a sentence. "nF#kV)/h7`"	Typecloze	Cloze Grammar::Japanese::番外（JLPT範囲外）::08 関西弁	(Kansai) もう{{c1::行かなあかん::行く · must (Kansai)}}。	I've got to go.	Form	なあかん means 'must': 行かなあかん = 行かなければならない. It's the Kansai なきゃ. もう = already, now. The ending is shortened from なければあかん. It's heard constantly in Osaka.
